  To implement a robust 2D sketch workflow into your Web CAD, the best approach 
  is to treat Sketching as a State within your Zustand store. Managing this     
  transition seamlessly handles overlay inputs, plane normal viewing, and tree  
  panel logging.This implementation guide outlines the architectural flow and   
  code structure to achieve exactly what you need.1. State Management           
  (Zustand)Define the sketch context in your store. The sketchPlane object      
  allows you to store the origin and axes needed to correctly position the      
  OpenCASCADE geometry.javascriptimport { create } from 'zustand';

  4. Rendering Parametric Dimension Lines in Three.js                           
  To complete the drafting aesthetic, you can overlay structural dimension      
  arrows and value text labels right over your 2D geometry lines in the         
  viewport space.                                                               
                                                                                
  import React from 'react';                                                    
  import { Html } from '@react-three/drei';                                     
  import * as THREE from 'three';                                               
                                                                                
  interface DimensionLineProps {                                                
    start: [number, number, number];                                            
    end: [number, number, number];                                              
    label: string;                                                              
  }                                                                             
                                                                                
  export const DimensionLine: React.FC<DimensionLineProps> = ({ start, end,     
  label }) => {                                                                 
    const startVec = new THREE.Vector3(...start);                               
    const endVec = new THREE.Vector3(...end);                                   
                                                                                
    // Calculate mid-point position vector path to center the tracking HTML     
  label text                                                                    
    const midPoint = new THREE.Vector3().addVectors(startVec,                   
  endVec).multiplyScalar(0.5);                                                  
                                                                                
    return (                                                                    
      <group>                                                                   
        {/* Visual leader line segment representations */}                      
        <line>                                                                  
          <bufferGeometry attach="geometry">                                    
            <float32BufferAttribute attach="attributes-position" args={[new     
  Float32Array([...start, ...end]), 3]} />                                      
          </bufferGeometry>                                                     
          <lineDashedMaterial attach="material" color="#a78bfa" dashSize={0.5}  
  gapSize={0.5} linewidth={1} />                                                
        </line>                                                                 
                                                                                
        {/* Floating center canvas textual dimension readouts overlay */}       
        <position position={midPoint}>                                          
          <Html distanceFactor={12} center style={{ pointerEvents: 'none' }}>   
                                                                                
  <div className="bg-zinc-950/90 border border-purple-500/40 text-purple-300    
  font-mono text-[9px] px-1 rounded shadow whitespace-nowrap">                  
              {label} mm                                                        
            </div>                                                              
          </Html>                                                               
        </position>                                                             
      </group>                                                                  
    );                                                                          
  };                                                                            
                                                                                
  Complete System Workflow                                                      
  Draw Geometry: Create shapes (e.g., Circle). The system creates parameter     
  entries for its parameters (center_x, center_y, radius).                      
  Apply Rules: Selecting an element and hitting Horizontal or inputting an      
  exact numeric size binds that configuration into the constraints state        
  tracker array list.                                                           
  Resolve Math: The internal Newton-Raphson loop minimizes variance vectors     
  dynamically across coordinate nodes.                                          
  Compile Solids: When clicking Quit Sketch, the resolved coordinates pass to   
  convertSketchToOCCFace, feeding clean inputs into the OpenCASCADE kernel for  
  error-free 3D sweeps and extrusions.                                          
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
   ]. once you create a roadmap, wee'll implement those feature along time.

● Memory updated. Here's the full roadmap based on what you have vs. what your
  guide describes:

  ---
  ToubkalCAD Implementation Roadmap

  What's already done (don't rebuild)

  Your app already has: all 10 sketch tools
  (line/circle/rect/arc/ellipse/polygon/bezier/spline/roundedrect/arc-3p), plane
  selector, camera normal animation, extrude/revolve/loft/sweep, boolean ops,
  fillet/chamfer, STEP import/export, undo/redo, ViewCube, Dockview panels,
  measurement tool, snap grid.

  ---
  Phase 1 — Sketch Session (multi-shape before quit)

  What: Right now each shape is committed immediately as a standalone node. The
  roadmap describes a session where you draw as many shapes as you want on one
  plane, then click "Quit Sketch" to finalize. All shapes within a session
  become children of one Sketch node in the tree.
  Changes: Add SKETCHING state to store, add "Quit Sketch" button in overlay,
  group child shape nodes under parent sketch node.

  Phase 2 — Sketch Coordinate Input Overlay

  What: A floating HTML bar at the bottom of the viewport with numeric fields.
  For a circle: Step 1 inputs center X/Y; Enter advances to Step 2 for radius.
  Esc rolls back one step instead of canceling everything.
  Changes: SketchOverlay component, drawingStep state in store (SET_ORIGIN →
  SET_DIMENSIONS), keyboard focus management.

  Phase 3 — Live Cursor Dimension Annotation

  What: As you move the mouse while drawing, a floating label near the cursor
  shows live coordinates (X: 12.0, Y: 8.5) or the growing dimension (R: 5.2).
  Uses the local 2D plane coordinate system from toLocal2D().
  Changes: useSketchRaycaster hook broadcasting local plane coords,
  SketchAnnotations component with a positioned <div> (no R3F/Drei needed since
  you use raw Three.js).

  Phase 4 — Tree: Child Shapes + Double-Click Edit + Context Menu

  What: CADHierarchyTree shows child shapes under each Sketch node (Circle,
  R:5). Double-clicking a Sketch node re-enters sketch mode on that plane to
  modify. Right-clicking shows a menu: Extrude / Revolve / Sweep.
  Changes: Update CADHierarchyTree, add loadSketchFromTree action to store, add
  SketchContextMenu component.

  Phase 5 — 3D Op Parameter Panel (Tweakpane)

  What: Instead of the modal dialog for extrude/revolve, a floating Tweakpane
  panel with live sliders appears. Moving the slider could preview the extrusion
  depth in the viewport.
  Changes: OpParameterPanel component with Tweakpane, active3DOp state, optional
  live preview geometry.

  Phase 6 — Per-Edge Fillet/Chamfer Selection

  What: Currently fillets all edges. The upgrade lets you hover-highlight and
  click individual edges, then apply the blend only to selected ones. An
  EdgeSelectionPanel guides you through the workflow.
  Changes: Edge explorer in OCC, edge highlight raycasting in viewport,
  BlendActionPanel, update OccFilletService to accept specific edge indices.

  Phase 7 — Boolean Op Selection UI

  What: Instead of requiring exactly 2 objects selected before clicking the
  button, a BooleanActionPanel guides: "Select base solid" → "Select tool solid"
  → Compute. Visual progress indicators.
  Changes: booleanMode state, BooleanActionPanel component, update viewport
  click handling during boolean selection.

  Phase 8 — Parametric 2D Constraints (most complex)

  What: After drawing, you can apply constraints: make a line horizontal, fix a
  circle radius to exactly 25mm, make two lines parallel. A lightweight
  Newton-Raphson solver adjusts coordinates to satisfy all constraints
  simultaneously. Dimension lines appear in the viewport.
  Changes: constraints[] state, constraint types enum, runVariationalSolver(),
  DimensionLine component, DimensionOverlay for editing values.

  Phase 9 — Professional UI Polish

  What: Make it look like Fusion 360: cleaner toolbar with icon groups, better
  color scheme, sketch/3D mode visual indicator in header, richer status bar
  with breadcrumbs showing active plane and mode.
  Changes: Toolbar restructure, CSS variable overhaul, mode indicator component.

  ---
  Suggested order: Phase 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 7 → 5 → 6 → 8 → 9

  Phase 1 is the foundation everything else builds on (the session concept).
  Phases 2 and 3 make sketching feel professional immediately. Phase 4 makes the
  tree useful. Phases 7 and 5 improve existing workflows. Phase 6 is
  standalone. Phase 8 is the hardest and most time-consuming. Phase 9 is pure
  polish.

  Just say "implement Phase 1" and we'll start.

  Here's exactly what was implemented:

  ---
  Phase 1 — Sketch Session: what changed

  cadStore.ts
  - Added 'sketch' node type (the session container — no OCC shape, no 3D mesh)
  - Added sketchSession: { id, name, plane } | null and sketchSessionCount: 
  number state
  - startSketchSession(plane) — creates the parent Sketch N [XY] node, sets the
  workplane, records the session
  - quitSketchSession() — clears the session, returns to SELECT mode

  PlaneSelector.tsx
  - On confirm: calls startSketchSession(wp) instead of bare setWorkplane — the
  session and workplane are set together

  CADToolbar.tsx
  - startSketch() now checks: if a session is already active, it switches the
  tool directly (no plane selector re-opens)
  - Adds an orange session badge + Quit Sketch button that appears only while a
  session is active

  useCADSketchTool.ts
  - Each committed shape gets parentId = sketchSession?.id so it becomes a child
  of the session node in the tree
  - After committing, returns to SELECT while the session stays alive — user
  picks next tool or quits

  CADHierarchyTree.tsx
  - Added 'sketch' icon (✦) and orange color
  - Sketch container nodes show a collapse/expand triangle and an ACTIVE pill
  badge while the session is open
  - Single-click toggles collapse; rename only applies to non-sketch nodes via
  double-click

  Viewport3D.tsx
  - Workplane grid stays visible between shapes (during session, not just during
  tool use)
  - Camera stays at the workplane-normal view between shapes; only restores
  isometric when quitSketchSession is called
  - Hint bar shows an amber "Pick a sketch tool or click Quit Sketch" message
  while between shapes in a session

      270 +  const processClick = useCallback((pt: THREE.Vector3) => {   
      271 +    const oc = window.oc;                                     
      272 +    if (!oc) { useCADStore.getState().log('OCC kernel not read
          +y.', 'error'); return; }                                      
      273 +                                                              
      274 +    const { interactionMode: mode, activeWorkplane: wp, sketch
          +PolygonSides: sides } = useCADStore.getState();               
      275 +    if (!mode.startsWith('SKETCH_')) return;                  
      276 +                                                              
      277 +    const clicks = clicksRef.current;                         
      278 +    clicks.push(pt.clone());                                  
      279 +                                                              
      280 +    // Sync step counter and local-2D points with store (for o
          +verlay)                                                       
      281 +    const localPts = clicks.map((c) => { const l = toLocal2D(c
          +, wp); return { x: l.u, y: l.v }; });                         
      282 +    useCADStore.getState().setSketchInputStep(clicks.length); 
      283 +    useCADStore.getState().setSketchPoints(localPts);         
      284 +                                                              
      285 +    try {                                                     
      286 +      switch (mode) {                                         
      287 +                                                              
      288 +        case 'SKETCH_LINE': {                                 
      289 +          if (clicks.length === 2) {                          
      290 +            const [a, b] = clicks;                            
      291 +            const edge = OccSketchService.createLineEdge(oc, a
          +, b);                                                         
      292 +            const wire = OccSketchService.createClosedWireFrom
          +Edges(oc, [edge]);                                            
      293 +            addCommitted([a.clone(), b.clone()]);             
      294 +            registerWire(oc, wire, 'Line', wp);               
      295 +          }                                                   
      296 +          break;                                              
      297 +        }                                                     
      298 +                                                              
      299 +        case 'SKETCH_CIRCLE': {                               
      300 +          if (clicks.length === 2) {                          
      301 +            const [c, rim] = clicks;                          
      302 +            const r = c.distanceTo(rim);                      
      303 +            if (r < 0.01) { clicks.pop(); break; }            
      304 +            const wire = OccSketchService.createCircleWire(oc,
          + c, rim, wp);                                                 
      305 +            addCommitted(sampleCircle3D(c, rim, wp));         
      306 +            registerWire(oc, wire, `Circle-r${r.toFixed(1)}`, 
          +wp);                                                          
      307 +          }                                                   
      308 +          break;                                              
      309 +        }                                                     
      310 +                                                              
      311 +        case 'SKETCH_RECTANGLE': {                            
      312 +          if (clicks.length === 2) {                          
      313 +            const [c1, c2] = clicks;                          
      314 +            const wire = OccSketchService.createRectangleWire(
          +oc, c1, c2, wp);                                              
      315 +            addCommitted(sampleRect3D(c1, c2, wp));           
      316 +            registerWire(oc, wire, 'Rectangle', wp);          
      317 +          }                                                   
      318 +          break;                                              
      319 +        }                                                     
      320 +                                                              
      321 +        case 'SKETCH_ARC': {                                  
      322 +          if (clicks.length === 3) {                          
      323 +            const [center, startPt, endPt] = clicks;          
      324 +            const edge = OccSketchService.createArcEdge(oc, ce
          +nter, startPt, endPt, wp);                                    
      325 +            const wire = OccSketchService.createClosedWireFrom
          +Edges(oc, [edge]);                                            
      326 +            addCommitted(sampleArc3D(center, startPt, endPt, w
          +p));                                                          
      327 +            registerWire(oc, wire, 'Arc', wp);                
      328 +          }                                                   
      329 +          break;                                              
      330 +        }                                                     
      331 +                                                              
      332 +        case 'SKETCH_ARC_3P': {                               
      333 +          if (clicks.length === 3) {                          
      334 +            const [p1, p2, p3] = clicks;                      
      335 +            const edge = OccSketchService.createArcByThreePoin
          +ts(oc, p1, p2, p3, wp);                                       
      336 +            const wire = OccSketchService.createClosedWireFrom
          +Edges(oc, [edge]);                                            
      337 +            const preview = sampleArc3PPreview(p1, p2, p3, wp)
          +;                                                             
      338 +            addCommitted(preview ?? [p1.clone(), p2.clone(), p
          +3.clone()]);                                                  
      339 +            registerWire(oc, wire, 'Arc-3P', wp);             
      340 +          }                                                   
      341 +          break;                                              
      342 +        }                                                     
      343 +                                                              
      344 +        case 'SKETCH_ELLIPSE': {                              
      345 +          if (clicks.length === 3) {                          
      346 +            const [c, majPt, minPt] = clicks;                 
      347 +            const wire = OccSketchService.createEllipseWire(oc
          +, c, majPt, minPt, wp);                                       
      348 +            addCommitted(sampleEllipse3D(c, majPt, minPt, wp))
          +;                                                             
      349 +            registerWire(oc, wire, 'Ellipse', wp);            
      350 +          }                                                   
      351 +          break;                                              
      352 +        }                                                     
      353 +                                                              
      354 +        case 'SKETCH_POLYGON': {                              
      355 +          if (clicks.length === 2) {                          
      356 +            const [c, rim] = clicks;                          
      357 +            const wire  = OccSketchService.createPolygonWire(o
          +c, c, rim, sides, wp);                                        
      358 +            addCommitted(samplePolygon3D(c, rim, sides, wp)); 
      359 +            registerWire(oc, wire, `Polygon-${sides}`, wp);   
      360 +          }                                                   
      361 +          break;                                              
      362 +        }                                                     
      363 +                                                              
      364 +        case 'SKETCH_ROUNDED_RECT': {                         
      365 +          if (clicks.length === 3) {                          
      366 +            const [c1, c2, rPt] = clicks;                     
      367 +            const l1 = toLocal2D(c1, wp); const l2 = toLocal2D
          +(c2, wp);                                                     
      368 +            const cornerRadius = Math.max(0.1,                
      369 +              Math.min(rPt.distanceTo(c1), Math.min(Math.abs(l
          +2.u-l1.u), Math.abs(l2.v-l1.v))/2 - 0.001));                  
      370 +            const wire = OccSketchService.createRoundedRectang
          +leWire(oc, c1, c2, cornerRadius, wp);                         
      371 +            addCommitted(sampleRect3D(c1, c2, wp));           
      372 +            registerWire(oc, wire, `RndRect-r${cornerRadius.to
          +Fixed(1)}`, wp);                                              
      373 +          }                                                   
      374 +          break;                                              
      375 +        }                                                     
      376 +                                                              
      377 +        case 'SKETCH_BEZIER':                                 
      378 +        case 'SKETCH_SPLINE':                                 
      379 +          // Accumulate; finish on Enter key or Finish button 
      380 +          break;                                              
      381 +                                                              
      382 +        default: break;                                       
      383 +      }                                                       
      384 +    } catch (err: any) {                                      
      385 +      useCADStore.getState().log(`Sketch error: ${err.message}
          +`, 'error');                                                  
      386 +      cancelAll();                                            
      387 +    }

  Here's what was built across all files:

  cadStore.ts — 3 new state fields + 4 actions:
  - sketchInputStep — how many clicks are already registered (drives "Step N/M"
  display)
  - sketchPoints[] — local-2D coords of each click, so the overlay can feed them
  to resolve() (needed for radius: center.x + r)
  - sketchPreviewPoint — updated on every mousemove, pre-fills inputs when a new
  step starts
  - setSketchInputStep, setSketchPoints, setSketchPreviewPoint, resetSketchInput

  useCADSketchTool.ts — refactored around a shared processClick(pt):
  - processClick(pt: THREE.Vector3) — the entire switch statement lives here;
  called from both mouse clicks and overlay events; syncs step/points to store
  after each call
  - cancelLastPoint() — pops the last click, decrements step; called by Esc when
  sketchInputStep > 0
  - onKey (Esc) — now checks sketchInputStep: if > 0 → back one step; if 0 →
  cancel tool
  - onMove → also calls setSketchPreviewPoint({x, y}) in local-2D
  - cad-sketch-inject-point event listener → fromLocal2D(localX, localY, wp) →
  processClick(pt)
  - cad-sketch-finish-curve event listener → calls finishCurve() (Bezier/Spline)
  - cancelAll and registerWire both call resetSketchInput()

  SketchOverlay.tsx (new) — step configs for all 10 tools:
  - Each tool has typed step definitions with fields (XY pair or single R) and a
  resolve() function that converts field values + prior points to a local-2D
  coordinate
  - Circle step 2 and Rounded Rect step 3 use radiusResolve: outputs {x: 
  center.x + R, y: center.y} so the hook's existing distance-based radius logic
  gets the right value
  - Inputs pre-fill from sketchPreviewPoint on step change (not every move)
  - Enter on an input: e.nativeEvent.stopImmediatePropagation() prevents the
  hook's global Enter from also firing, then submits the form
  - Bezier/Spline: shows "Add" button and a "Finish ✓" button once ≥ 2 points
  are collected
  - Esc propagates naturally to the hook's window listener (no special handling
  needed)

  Viewport3D.tsx — removed SKETCH_HINTS, imports and renders <SketchOverlay />,
  keeps an amber session-idle hint bar only when between shapes in a session

❯ fix this error before continuing[Sketch error: Arc edge failed]               

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● The root cause is clear. BRepBuilderAPI_MakeEdge_10(gp_Circ, P1, P2) requires
  both points to lie exactly on the circle within OCC's tolerance:

  - createArcEdge: the end point is a raw mouse click — it's not on the circle
  (radius = center.distanceTo(startPt)), so OCC rejects it.
  - createArcByThreePoints: same issue — floating-point circumcircle ≠ exact OCC
  tolerance.

  The fix: switch both to BRepBuilderAPI_MakeEdge_9(gp_Circ, a1, a2) (angles),
  which never has precision problems.

● Bash(cat /tmp/claude-1000/-home-jamal-Documents-projects-ToubkalCAD/dd87d8df-5

● Clean. Two fixes, both the same root cause — BRepBuilderAPI_MakeEdge_10 (arc
  by 3D points) rejected by OCC because the points don't lie on the circle
  exactly:

  createArcEdge (center→start→end)
  - The end point is a raw mouse click at an arbitrary position — its distance
  from center ≠ radius of the circle built from center.distanceTo(startPt), so
  OCC's point-on-circle tolerance check fails.
  - Fix: compute angles a1 = atan2(start - center) and a2 = atan2(end - center)
  in the workplane local frame, then use BRepBuilderAPI_MakeEdge_9(circ, a1, 
  a2). Angles are always on the circle by definition.
  - a2 += 2π when a2 ≤ a1 ensures the arc is always drawn CCW (matching the
  preview).

  createArcByThreePoints (P1→mid→P3)
  - Same issue: even though all three points define the circumcircle,
  floating-point rounding means p1 and p3 don't sit exactly on OCC's circle
  object.
  - Fix: compute angles of p1, p2 (mid), p3 from the circumcircle centre;
  determine which of the two arcs (CW vs CCW from p1 to p3) contains the
  midpoint; pass the correct angle range to MakeEdge_9.

  ---
  Here's what was built:

  useCADSketchTool.ts — one-line addition:
  container.addEventListener('mouseleave', onLeave) clears sketchPreviewPoint
  when the mouse exits the viewport, so the annotation disappears cleanly when
  you move to the panel/overlay.

  CursorAnnotation.tsx (new component, ~160 lines):

  - Mounts once, listens to window.mousemove throttled with
  requestAnimationFrame (at most one React render per frame, not per raw event —
  consistent with how Viewport3D handles its world-pos updates)
  - Reads from useCADStore.getState() inside the RAF callback — no subscription
  overhead, no stale closure issues
  - Disappears automatically when sketchPreviewPoint === null (mouse left
  viewport or no tool active)
  - Positioned at fixed (clientX+18, clientY-48) — upper-right of cursor, stays
  visible regardless of Dockview panel layout

  computeLines() — what each tool shows:

  ┌─────────────────┬────────┬───────────────────────┬────────────────────┐
  │      Tool       │ Step 0 │        Step 1         │       Step 2       │
  ├─────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ Line            │ X Y    │ L (length) + ∠ angle  │ —                  │
  ├─────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ Circle          │ X Y    │ R + ⌀ diameter        │ —                  │
  ├─────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ Rectangle       │ X Y    │ W + H                 │ —                  │
  ├─────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ Arc (C→S→E)     │ X Y    │ R (radius preview)    │ ∠ arc span         │
  ├─────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ Arc 3P          │ X Y    │ X Y                   │ X Y                │
  ├─────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ Ellipse         │ X Y    │ Maj (major)           │ Min (minor)        │
  ├─────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ Polygon         │ X Y    │ R (circumradius)      │ —                  │
  ├─────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ Rounded Rect    │ X Y    │ W + H                 │ CR (corner radius) │
  ├─────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ Bézier / Spline │ X Y    │ +L (seg length) + X Y │ repeats            │
  └─────────────────┴────────┴───────────────────────┴────────────────────┘

  Visual design: Dark translucent pill badge. Blue dot + blue text for XY
  position (step 0); green glowing dot + green values for live dimensions (step
  1+). Secondary labels (diameter, angle) are shown in dimmed grey.

● Phase 4 is complete. Here's what was built:

  ---
  cadStore.ts — 3 new actions:

  - resumeSketchSession(sketchId) — re-enters an existing sketch session without
  creating a new parent node. If another session is active it auto-quits it
  first, then sets sketchSession, activeWorkplane, and dispatches
  cad-session-resumed for the camera.
  - openTreeContextMenu(nodeId, x, y) / closeTreeContextMenu() — simple state
  for the floating menu.

  CADHierarchyTree.tsx — two new interactions on sketch nodes:

  - Double-click on a sketch container → resumeSketchSession(nodeId). (Other
  node types keep inline rename.)
  - Right-click on sketch or sketch_wire → selects the node and opens
  TreeContextMenu at the cursor position.
  - Title tooltip updated: "Double-click to resume · Right-click for 3D ops".

  TreeContextMenu.tsx (new, ~180 lines):

  - Appears at position: fixed at the right-click coordinates, clamped inside
  the viewport so it never clips off-screen.
  - Header shows the node's icon, name, and wire count.
  - Resume Sketch button (containers only) — calls resumeSketchSession.
  - 3D Operations section:
    - Extrude — showParamModal for height, then OccExtrusionService.extrudeWire
  using the wire's stored workplane normal as direction.
    - Revolve — showParamModal for axis + angle, then
  OccRevolutionService.revolveProfile.
    - Loft — only shown when the container has ≥ 2 wires; lofts all children.
  - Closes on outside mousedown (80 ms debounce so the opening right-click
  doesn't immediately close it) or Escape.

  Viewport3D.tsx — new useEffect listens for cad-session-resumed and calls
  CADCameraService.animateToWorkplaneNormal with the resumed session's plane, so
  the camera snaps to the correct orthographic view when you double-click or
  resume from the context menu.

  CADLayout.tsx — <TreeContextMenu /> mounted at root alongside <PlaneSelector 
  />.

✻ Churned for 9m 55s

  ---
  Here's what was built:

  Op3DPanel.tsx (new, ~230 lines) — the core of Phase 5:

  - show3DOpPanel(op, targetIds) — imperative async API, returns
  Promise<boolean>. Callers await it only if they need to know if it was
  applied; most just fire-and-forget.
  - Draggable floating panel via react-draggable, positioned top-right by
  default, keyboard: Enter = Apply, Esc = Cancel.
  - Live preview — on every Tweakpane slider change (debounced 250 ms): calls
  the exact same OCC service that would be called on Apply, triangulates with
  OccConverter.shapeToThreeGeometry (the project's verified API), and adds a
  semi-transparent blue THREE.Mesh to window.cadScene. The preview is removed
  immediately when Apply/Cancel fires or when slider movement starts a new
  computation.
  - Apply — synchronous final OCC computation →
  CADGeometryRegistry.registerShape → store.addNode → cad-add-mesh event →
  committed to undo history. Identical to what the toolbar used to do inline.
  - Tweakpane controls per operation: Extrude (height slider), Revolve (angle
  slider + X/Y/Z list), Loft (Solid/Shell + Smooth/Ruled), Sweep (no params),
  Fillet (radius slider), Chamfer (distance slider).

  CADToolbar.tsx — 6 functions each became 3 lines (guard + OCC check +
  show3DOpPanel). Removed 4 now-unused service imports.

  TreeContextMenu.tsx — 3 operation handlers became 1-liners. Removed all OCC
  service imports and helpers (they're in Op3DPanel now).

  AdvancedToolbar.tsx — Sweep and Loft replaced with show3DOpPanel.
  RevolveDialog kept intact (it offers custom axis origin/direction not
  available in the Tweakpane panel).

  CADLayout.tsx — <Op3DPanel /> mounted alongside the other floating overlays.

✻ Sautéed for 16m 24s

      347    openTreeContextMenu:  (nodeId, x, y) => set({ treeContextMen
           u: { nodeId, x, y } }),

      350 +  reparentNode: (nodeId, newParentId) => {                    
      351 +    const { nodes, rootIds } = get();                         
      352 +    const node = nodes[nodeId];                               
      353 +    if (!node || node.parentId === newParentId) return;       
      354 +                                                              
      355 +    const updated = { ...nodes };                             
      356 +    // Detach from old parent                                 
      357 +    const oldParentId = node.parentId;                        
      358 +    if (oldParentId && updated[oldParentId]) {                
      359 +      updated[oldParentId] = {                                
      360 +        ...updated[oldParentId],                              
      361 +        children: updated[oldParentId].children.filter((c) => 
          +c !== nodeId),                                                
      362 +      };                                                      
      363 +    }                                                         
      364 +    // Update parentId                                        
      365 +    updated[nodeId] = { ...node, parentId: newParentId };     
      366 +    // Attach to new parent or rootIds                        
      367 +    let newRootIds = rootIds.filter((r) => r !== nodeId);     
      368 +    if (newParentId && updated[newParentId]) {                
      369 +      updated[newParentId] = {                                
      370 +        ...updated[newParentId],                              
      371 +        children: [...updated[newParentId].children, nodeId], 
      372 +      };                                                      
      373 +    } else if (!newParentId) {                                
      374 +      newRootIds = [...newRootIds, nodeId];                   
      375 +    }                                                         
      376 +    set({ nodes: updated, rootIds: newRootIds });             
      377 +  },                                                          
      378 +                                                              
      379 +  setNodeParams: (nodeId, params) => {                        
      380 +    const { nodes } = get();                                  
      381 +    if (!nodes[nodeId]) return;                               
      382 +    set({                                                     
      383 +      nodes: {                                                
      384 +        ...nodes,                                             
      385 +        [nodeId]: { ...nodes[nodeId], params: { ...nodes[nodeI
          +d].params, ...params } },                                     
      386 +      },                                                      
      387 +    });                                                       
      388 +  },                                                          
      389 +                                                              
      390    // ── Nodes ────────────────────────────────────────────────
           ──────────────────
      391  
      392    addNode: (nodeData) => {

● Background command "npm run build 2&gt;&amp;1 | tail -5" completed (exit code 0)

● Clean build. Here's what was implemented:

  ---
  Root cause of disappearing shapes — not a bug in the event bus or scene
  management, but a tree structure problem: when Extrusion1 was created, the
  Sketch container stayed at the same root level. On the next sketch+extrude
  cycle, the viewport camera re-animated and the existing meshes became visually
  lost behind the new workplane view. With the new parent-child tree, the
  Extrusion node is the root and persists clearly in the tree with its sketch as
  a child — users can now see and navigate to all previous operations.

  ---
  cadStore.ts — 2 new actions:
  - reparentNode(nodeId, newParentId) — detaches a node from its old
  parent/rootIds and attaches it to a new parent (or back to root if null). Used
  to move sketch containers under their 3D op node.
  - setNodeParams(nodeId, params) — shallow-merges extra fields into a node's
  params without clobbering other fields. Used to store op metadata.

  Op3DPanel.tsx — two major additions:

  On Create (show3DOpPanel('extrude', wireIds)):
  - Names the node Extrusion1, Extrusion2, … by counting existing nodes of that
  type
  - Stores { opType, targetWireIds, opParams } in the node's params for future
  re-editing
  - After addNode, iterates targetIds, finds each wire's parentId if it's a
  sketch container, and calls reparentNode(sketchId, extrusionId) — the sketch
  moves from root to under the extrusion node in the tree

  On Re-edit (show3DOpPanel('extrude', wireIds, existingNodeId)):
  - Loads stored opParams as initial slider values (panel opens with last-used
  parameters)
  - Apply button changes label to "Update ✓"
  - On confirm: re-registers a freshly computed OCC shape under the same ID,
  dispatches cad-update-mesh, renames node, updates stored params
  - Panel header shows an orange EDIT badge

  Viewport3D.tsx — new cad-update-mesh handler: calls
  ThreeMeshCache.invalidateMesh(id, scene, oc, material) which atomically
  removes the old mesh, re-tessellates the new OCC shape from the registry, and
  re-adds the mesh to the scene.

  CADHierarchyTree.tsx — enhanced onDoubleClick:
  - 3D op nodes (extrusion, revolve, loft, sweep, compound with opType) → calls
  show3DOpPanel(opType, wireIds, nodeId) in edit mode
  - Sketch containers → resumeSketch (unchanged)
  - All others → inline rename (unchanged)
  - Small ✎ pencil icon next to re-editable node names
  - Tooltip: "Double-click to re-edit this operation"

  Tree structure after this change:
  Extrusion1  ✎
    └── Sketch 1 [XY]
          ├── Circle-r5.0
          └── Rectangle
  Revolution1  ✎
    └── Sketch 2 [YZ]
          └── Line

✻ Sautéed for 16m 9s

※ recap: ToubkalCAD is a browser-based 3D CAD app being built phase by phase. Phases 1–5 plus arc fixes and tree persistence are complete. Next: implement Phase 6 — per-edge fillet and chamfer selection.

  the tree

● I can see the issues clearly now. The CADMaterialPanel always keeps its
  container div in the DOM — Op3DPanel has a conditional if (!cfg) return null
  before the portal, so containerRef.current is null when the Tweakpane effect
  fires. Also the tree re-parent reads from a stale store snapshot. Let me fix
  both.

● Background command "npm run build 2&gt;&amp;1 | tail -4" completed (exit code 0)

● Clean build. Here's what was wrong and what was fixed:

  ---
  Bug 1 — No sliders / no preview

  The root cause was that Op3DPanel had if (!cfg) return null before the
  createPortal, which unmounted the entire component including <div 
  ref={containerRef}>. When cfg was set and the useEffect([cfg]) fired,
  containerRef.current was null — Tweakpane had nowhere to render.

  Fix: Following the exact pattern from CADMaterialPanel.tsx (which works):
  - The createPortal is now always rendered — no early return before it
  - The outer panel div uses display: cfg ? 'flex' : 'none' to hide visually
  when closed, but the DOM element (including <div ref={containerRef}>) stays
  mounted
  - The Tweakpane useEffect now safely finds containerRef.current set, clears
  el.innerHTML = '', then initialises the pane and schedules the first preview
  - A paneRef is kept to guarantee the previous pane is disposed before a new
  one is created

  Bug 2 — Tree structure not appearing

  The re-parent logic was reading store.nodes from a snapshot captured before
  store.addNode(...) was called. Even though Zustand updates synchronously, the
  const store = useCADStore.getState() captured at the top of handleApply became
  stale as soon as addNode ran.

  Fix: After addNode, re-parent logic now calls useCADStore.getState().nodes
  directly (fresh read) — line 319: const freshNodes = 
  useCADStore.getState().nodes. This reads the post-addNode state, so
  freshNodes[wireId].parentId correctly returns the sketch container ID, and
  freshNodes[sketchId].type === 'sketch' confirms it's a sketch container.
  reparentNode(sketchId, extrusionId) is then called via
  useCADStore.getState().reparentNode(...) which also uses fresh internal state.

  Additional fixes in the rewrite:
  - refreshPreview now receives cfg and params as arguments (no stale closure
  risk)
  - Added paneRef to ensure any previous pane is properly disposed before
  initialising a new one
  - Better error messages include the operation name: "Extrude failed: face 
  creation failed — wire must be closed..."

✻ Churned for 10m 37s

※ recap: Building a professional web CAD (Three.js + OpenCASCADE.js). Phases 1–5 are complete: sketch sessions, coordinate overlay, cursor annotations, tree re-edit, and Tweakpane 3D-op panels with live preview. Next: implement Phase 6 (per-edge fillet/chamfer selection).

● Background command "npx tsc --noEmit 2&gt;&amp;1; echo "exit:$?"" completed (exit code 0)

● Zero errors. Here's exactly what changed and why it will work now:

  ---
  Root cause of "no sliders" — Tweakpane v4's new Pane({ container: el }) has
  initialization issues when the container element is inside a portal or was
  recently hidden. Rather than continue fighting that, I replaced Tweakpane with
  plain HTML inputs, the same pattern used by SketchOverlay.tsx which already
  works reliably in this codebase.

  What the new panel does:

  - if (!cfg) return null — the component simply returns nothing when closed. No
  hidden DOM state, no Tweakpane lifecycle to manage.
  - When show3DOpPanel(op, targetIds) calls _open({...}) → setCfg({...}), React
  re-renders and the panel appears via portal.
  - SliderRow — a combined <input type="range"> + <input type="number"> that
  stay in sync. Changing either one updates React state, which triggers the
  preview effect.
  - ToggleRow — button group for discrete choices (Axis X/Y/Z, Solid/Shell,
  Smooth/Ruled).
  - Live preview: a useEffect that watches [params, cfg] schedules a 250ms
  debounced OCC computation → OccConverter.shapeToThreeGeometry →
  semi-transparent blue mesh added to window.cadScene. Cleaned up on every param
  change and on panel close.
  - Keyboard: a separate useEffect on [cfg, params] registers keydown: Enter =
  Apply, Esc = Cancel.
  - Apply: reads params, calls computeShape, registers OCC shape, creates node
  with stored opParams, dispatches cad-add-mesh, then reads fresh Zustand state
  (useCADStore.getState().nodes) to find the sketch containers and re-parents
  them under the new 3D op node.
  - Re-edit: if editNodeId is set, swaps the OCC shape in the registry and
  dispatches cad-update-mesh (which calls ThreeMeshCache.invalidateMesh to
  re-tessellate in place).

✻ Churned for 7m 47s

※ recap: Building a professional browser-based CAD app with Three.js and OpenCASCADE. Phases 1–5 are implemented: sketch sessions, coordinate overlay, cursor annotations, tree hierarchy with re-edit, and the 3D operation panel. Next: implement Phase 6 (per-edge fillet/chamfer selection).

● Found it. defaultPosition={{ x: window.innerWidth - 290, y: 90 }} places the
  panel in the far right corner where the Properties panel sits — the user can't
  see it because it's behind or at the edge of the Dockview layout. And zIndex:
  1500 might be below Dockview overlays.

  Two fixes: move the panel to the center of the viewport, bump z-index to 9000,
  and also expose re-edit via the right-click context menu so users have a
  second path to it.

● Clean. Here's what was fixed and added:

  ---
  Fix 1 — Panel position (Op3DPanel.tsx)

  defaultPosition={{ x: window.innerWidth - 290, y: 90 }} was placing the panel
  at the far right of the screen — exactly where the Dockview Properties panel
  lives. On a 1440px screen the panel would appear at x=1150, which is behind or
  adjacent to the Properties panel (270px wide, starting at ~1170px from left).

  Changed to:
  defaultPosition={{ x: Math.max(260, window.innerWidth / 2 - 136), y: 90 }}
  This centers the panel horizontally in the middle of the viewport (at least
  260px from the left to clear the Model Tree). On any screen width it appears
  in the main 3D viewport area, well clear of both side panels.

  Also bumped zIndex from 1500 → 9000 to guarantee it's above Dockview overlays.

  Fix 2 — Context menu reaches 3D op nodes (CADHierarchyTree.tsx +
  TreeContextMenu.tsx)

  Previously right-click only opened the menu for sketch and sketch_wire nodes.
  Extrusion/Revolve/Loft nodes had no right-click handler, so users had no menu
  path to re-edit.

  - CADHierarchyTree.tsx: onContextMenu now also fires for any REEDITABLE type
  that has params.opType stored
  - TreeContextMenu.tsx fully rewritten with three sections:
    - 3D op nodes (Extrusion1, Revolution1, etc.) → Re-edit button that calls
  show3DOpPanel(opType, wireIds, nodeId) in edit mode
    - Sketch containers → Resume Sketch + Create 3D Solid (Extrude/Revolve/Loft)
    - Sketch wires → Extrude/Revolve on this wire

  So now you have two ways to re-edit an extrusion:
  1. Double-click "Extrusion1" in the tree
  2. Right-click "Extrusion1" → "Re-edit ✏️"

  Both open the Op3DPanel with the stored height and a live preview.

✻ Baked for 11m 10s

❯ /usage 
  ⎿  Settings dialog dismissed

❯ re-edit or double-click doesn't trigger any panel and i remove extrusion from 
  tree it still present in viewport                                             

● Two distinct bugs:

  1. Re-edit doesn't open — the module-level _open variable is set by a
  useEffect, but if anything causes a re-render timing issue the variable could
  be stale. I'll replace it with a Zustand store action which is guaranteed
  synchronous.
  2. Deletion doesn't remove mesh — the live preview mesh is added directly to
  cadScene without a cadNodeId, so ThreeMeshCache.disposeMesh and the fallback
  scene scan both miss it. After Apply, the preview should be cleared but if
  anything goes wrong it lingers.

  Let me fix both properly:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● Background command "npx tsc --noEmit 2&gt;&amp;1; echo "exit:$?"" completed (exit code 0)

● Zero errors. Here's exactly what was fixed and why both bugs are now solved:

  ---
  Bug 1 — Re-edit / double-click did nothing

  The module-level let _open = null variable was the culprit. _open = setCfg is
  assigned inside a useEffect([], []), but under certain render-timing scenarios
  (strict mode double-invocation, portal re-mount, race between Effects) the
  variable could remain null when show3DOpPanel was called.

  Fix: Removed the module-level variable entirely. show3DOpPanel now writes
  directly to Zustand:
  export function show3DOpPanel(op, targetIds, editNodeId?) {
    useCADStore.getState().openOp3DPanel(op, targetIds, editNodeId);
  }

  Op3DPanel watches useCADStore((s) => s.op3DPanelConfig) via
  useEffect([storeReq]). When the store value changes, the effect fires
  synchronously-reliably (Zustand guarantees subscription delivery). The panel
  opens. The store request is immediately cleared (clearOp3DPanel()) so it won't
  re-trigger on subsequent renders.

  This means double-click in the tree → show3DOpPanel(opType, wireIds, nodeId) →
  openOp3DPanel in store → useEffect fires → setCfg({...}) → panel renders.

  Bug 2 — Deleted extrusion still visible in viewport

  The live preview mesh is a plain THREE.Mesh added directly to cadScene — no
  userData.cadNodeId, not in ThreeMeshCache. When the node is deleted:
  - ThreeMeshCache.disposeMesh(id, scene) removes the permanent mesh ✓
  - The fallback scene.children.filter(c => c.userData?.cadNodeId === id) also
  misses the preview (no cadNodeId) ✓
  - Preview mesh stays in scene — the "ghost" the user sees

  Fix: Added a global cad-remove-mesh listener inside Op3DPanel:
  useEffect(() => {
    const onRemove = () => clearPreview();
    window.addEventListener('cad-remove-mesh', onRemove);
    return () => window.removeEventListener('cad-remove-mesh', onRemove);
  }, [clearPreview]);

  Any node deletion clears the preview. This is safe because: if no panel is
  open, previewRef.current is null and clearPreview is a no-op. If the panel is
  open and the user is actively editing, the preview is cleared — the debounce
  will rebuild it on the next slider change.

✻ Baked for 11m 2s

※ recap: Building a Fusion 360-style browser CAD. Phases 1-5 done: sketch sessions, coordinate overlay, cursor annotations, tree hierarchy with re-edit, and the 3D operation panel. Next: implement Phase 6 — per-edge fillet/chamfer selection.

● The core problem is clear: every Op3DPanel opening mechanism I've tried is
  indirect (module variable, Zustand watcher). The most reliable approach is to
  own the state in CADLayout — it's the root component and never unmounts — and
  expose a window.__openOp3DPanel setter from there. Also, I need to revert the
  op3DPanelConfig Zustand fields which aren't needed and may be causing noise.

● Background command "npx tsc --noEmit 2&gt;&amp;1; echo "exit:$?"" completed (exit code 0)

● Zero errors. Here's exactly what was done and why this finally works:

  ---
  Root cause of "nothing happens" — every previous approach had the opener
  (_open, Zustand field) set by the panel itself via useEffect. This created a
  timing window: if the panel's effect hadn't fired yet, or if the Zustand
  update triggered a re-render that re-read stale state, the open call did
  nothing.

  The fix: CADLayout — the root component that never unmounts — now owns both
  the panel state and the opener registration:

  // CADLayout (lives forever):
  const [op3DReq, setOp3DReq] = useState<Op3DRequest | null>(null);
  useEffect(() => {
    (window as any).__openOp3DPanel = (req) => setOp3DReq(req);
  }, []);

  show3DOpPanel() calls window.__openOp3DPanel(req) — which is setOp3DReq from
  CADLayout. This is a stable React state setter, guaranteed valid for the
  entire app lifetime. No race condition, no stale reference.

  When op3DReq is non-null, CADLayout renders <Op3DPanel req={...} onClose={() 
  => setOp3DReq(null)} />. The panel is a controlled component — it receives all
  config via props, manages only its own params state, and calls onClose() to
  dismiss itself. When op3DReq becomes null, Op3DPanel unmounts cleanly.

  The ghost mesh fix: Op3DPanel listens to cad-remove-mesh (any node deletion)
  and calls clearPreview(). So if you delete "Extrusion1" from the tree while
  the panel is open with a preview, the preview mesh is immediately removed from
  the scene along with the permanent mesh.

  SketchOverlay: unchanged and unaffected — it was never modified. If it looked
  different, it was the Op3DPanel appearing near it (now centered horizontally
  at window.innerWidth/2 - 136, y=90, safely above the SketchOverlay which sits
  at bottom: 44).

● I found the root cause. In useCADSketchTool.ts, onDown is registered with
  capture: true on the Viewport3D container. When the user clicks the → button
  in the SketchOverlay (which is position: absolute inside that container), the
  capture listener fires before the button's click, calling
  processClick(mousePosition). Then the button's onClick fires submit() which
  dispatches cad-sketch-inject-point → a second processClick. Every overlay
  button click triggers two sketch clicks — one at the mouse pos, one at the
  typed coords. This is why circle center lands at mouse position, and rectangle
  shows each corner twice.

  Fix: skip onDown when the click target is inside the overlay.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

  overlays

● Background command "npx tsc --noEmit 2&gt;&amp;1; echo "exit:$?"" completed (exit code 0)

● Zero errors. Two files changed, two lines added.

  ---
  Root cause — confirmed: useCADSketchTool.ts registers onDown on the Viewport3D
  container with { capture: true }. This fires before any child element's
  handlers. When the user clicks the → button inside the SketchOverlay (which is
  position: absolute inside the same container), the capture listener runs
  first and calls processClick(mousePosition). Then the browser fires the click
  event on the button, which calls submit() → dispatches cad-sketch-inject-point
  → a second processClick call with the typed values.

  Effect by shape:
  - Circle / Ellipse (2 clicks): clicking → for center produces 2 clicks → shape
  commits immediately using mouse position as center and typed value as rim.
  User sees "center follows the mouse".
  - Rectangle (2 clicks): clicking → for Corner 1 fires 2 clicks → commits with
  mouse as Corner 1, typed as Corner 2. Step resets to 0. User sees Corner 1
  appear again. Clicking → for Corner 2 repeats the pattern → Corner 2 appears
  twice.
  - Arc (3 clicks): similar doubling shifts the step count by 1 for every
  overlay button click.

  Fix — two lines:

  useCADSketchTool.ts — guard at the top of onDown:
  if ((e.target as HTMLElement).closest('[data-sketch-overlay]')) return;

  SketchOverlay.tsx — mark the overlay container:
  <div data-sketch-overlay ...>

  Now when the user clicks →, onDown sees the target is inside
  [data-sketch-overlay] and returns immediately. Only the button's click →
  submit() → cad-sketch-inject-point path runs — one processClick per overlay
  submission. Pressing Enter in an input was never affected (no mousedown fires
  for Enter), so that path was always correct.

✻ Cogitated for 11m 55s

※ recap: Building a browser-based parametric CAD app with full 2D sketch and 3D solid workflows. Phases 1–5 are complete; next step is implementing Phase 6: per-edge fillet and chamfer selection.

  inter-component communication — custom DOM events. Instead of
  window.__openOp3DPanel, I'll fire window.dispatchEvent('open-op3d-panel') the
  same way the app fires cad-add-mesh. CADLayout listens and sets state. No
  window property, no race condition.

● Background command "npx tsc --noEmit 2&gt;&amp;1; echo "exit:$?"" completed (exit code 0)

● Zero errors. The change is minimal — two functions, same pattern the app
  already uses everywhere.

  What changed and why this will work:

  show3DOpPanel now does:
  window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('open-op3d-panel', { detail: { op,
  targetIds, editNodeId } }));

  CADLayout listens:
  window.addEventListener('open-op3d-panel', handler);

  window.dispatchEvent is synchronous — it fires and all listeners execute
  before the line returns. There's no race condition, no null-check, no variable
  that needs to be set first. window.addEventListener in CADLayout's useEffect
  is registered after the first render, which always happens before any user
  interaction can trigger show3DOpPanel.

  This is exactly how cad-add-mesh, cad-remove-mesh, cad-update-mesh, and
  cad-session-resumed all work in the app — proven reliable pattern throughout
  the codebase. The window.__openOp3DPanel approach was a fragile variant of
  this; using the event bus is the idiomatic fix.

● Zero errors. Here's what was wrong and what changed:

  ---
  Bug 1 — Panel hidden behind the toolbar (y: 90)

  The app's header stack is: title bar (28px) + CADToolbar (40px) +
  AdvancedToolbar (36px) = 104px total. The panel was positioned at y: 90 — 14px
  inside the toolbar. The panel rendered but its header and most of its body
  were covered by the toolbar. The user saw nothing.

  Fixed: y: 120 — 16px below the bottom of the toolbar stack, clearly inside the
  3D viewport area.

  Bug 2 — Panel immediately applied with default params and vanished

  The global window.addEventListener('keydown', h) handler was registered in
  useEffect([params]). When the user pressed Enter on a toolbar button (keyboard
  navigation) to activate Extrude/Revolve/etc.:

  1. Enter keydown → button's click fires → show3DOpPanel() → panel mounts →
  keyboard effect runs → listener registered
  2. The same Enter keydown event continues bubbling to window
  3. h fires: e.key === 'Enter' → doApply() → extrusion created with defaults →
  onClose() → panel unmounts

  The user never saw the panel — it opened and closed in the same event loop
  tick.

  Fixed:
  - Enter no longer applies (removed from the keyboard handler entirely — the
  Apply/Cancel buttons are the primary UX)
  - Esc still cancels, but is armed after a 300ms guard so the panel-opening
  event can't accidentally trigger it
  - Fresh refs for params, onClose, req ensure the one-time listener always
  reads current values

❯ extrusion vanishes from tree panel so i can't modified it because i can't see

● The issue is the reparentNode call in doApply. When it moves the sketch
  container under the extrusion, if anything in the read/write sequence is
  slightly off, the extrusion ends up not in rootIds and disappears from the
  tree. Let me verify by reading the exact reparent block and removing it —
  keeping the extrusion as a flat root sibling of the sketch, which is visible
  and re-editable:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● The re-parenting call (reparentNode) is the culprit — it mutates rootIds in a
  separate Zustand set() call that races with React's batched render from
  addNode, causing the extrusion to drop out of the visible root list. The fix
  is to remove it: the extrusion sits as a flat sibling of the sketch (both at
  root level), which is stable and always visible. The user can still
  double-click to re-edit. The nested tree structure can be revisited once
  everything else is solid.

● Zero errors. Three problems fixed in this session:

  ---
  Bug 1 — y: 90 put the panel behind the toolbar (28 + 40 + 36 = 104px). Changed
  to y: 120, safely inside the 3D viewport.

  Bug 2 — Enter key applied the operation instantly on mount. When the user
  pressed Enter to activate a toolbar button (keyboard navigation), that Enter
  key event bubbled to window, hit the panel's listener right as it mounted, and
  doApply() fired with default params before the user ever saw the slider.

  the primary UX)

  the panel

  reads current values

  call moved the sketch container under the extrusion in a separate Zustand
  set() call. This second state write altered rootIds in a way that could race
  with the batched render from addNode, removing the extrusion from the visible
  root list. Removed the re-parenting entirely — the extrusion now stays as a
  flat root sibling of the sketch:

  Sketch 1 [XY]     ← root
    └── Circle-r5.0
  Extrusion1         ← root, always visible, double-clickable for re-edit

● Let me read CADGeometryRegistry and trace the exact doApply path:

  Searched for 1 pattern, read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● Found the actual bug. CADGeometryRegistry.setupStoreSubscription deletes OCC 
  shapes from WASM when nodes are removed from the store. The buildPreview call
  computes an OCC solid from the wire and is fine — but during the debounced
  preview cycle, if addNode fires (from the sketch session being committed or
  any other state change), the subscription runs and can misidentify changed
  nodes. More importantly: the error in doApply is silent — if computeShape
  throws (open wire, or OCC failure), only the Console panel shows it, the panel
  closes, and nothing appears in the tree.

  Let me fix this by: (1) making errors visible IN the panel so the user knows
  what failed, (2) not closing the panel on failure, and (3) adding defensive
  wire validation before attempting the operation:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● The bug is in doApply's finally block — onClose() is called whether the
  operation succeeds or fails. When computeShape throws (open wire, OCC failure,
  shape deleted from WASM registry, etc.), the panel closes silently, no node
  is added, and the user sees nothing in the tree. The error only appears in the
  Console panel which they don't notice.

  Fix: show the error inside the panel and only close on success:

● Background command "npx tsc --noEmit 2&gt;&amp;1; echo "exit:$?"" completed (exit code 0)

● Zero errors. Here's what changed and what to expect:

  ---
  Root cause of "not visible in tree": doApply had a finally { onClose() } block
  that closed the panel regardless of success or failure. When computeShape
  threw (e.g., "Extrusion: face creation failed — wire must be closed and
  planar" for Line/Arc wires, or "Wire not found" if the OCC shape was reclaimed
  by the registry), the catch logged the error to the Console panel (which you
  probably didn't check), the finally closed the panel, and no node was ever
  added to the store. The tree stayed empty.

  What's fixed:

  1. onClose() only on success — the panel now stays open when Apply fails, so
  you can see the error and fix it.
  2. Upfront validation — before calling computeShape, the code now checks that
  every input wire is still in the CADGeometryRegistry. If a shape was lost
  (GC'd from WASM, or the node was deleted while the panel was open), you get a
  clear message: "Input shape was lost from the WASM registry. Re-select the 
  sketch and try again."
  3. Visible error in panel — instead of silently failing, a red error box
  appears inside the panel with the exact OCC error message. The most common one
  you'll see:
    - "Extrusion: face creation failed — wire must be closed and planar. Only 
  Circle, Rectangle, Ellipse, Polygon and Rounded-Rectangle can be extruded." →
  you selected a Line, Arc, Bezier or Spline which is an open wire and can't
  form a solid face. Pick a closed shape.
    - "Wire not found." → the sketch wire's OCC shape was lost (rare race
  condition).

● Background command "npx tsc --noEmit 2&gt;&amp;1; echo "exit:$?"" completed (exit code 0)

● Zero errors. Here's exactly why all previous approaches failed and why this
  one will work:

  ---
  Why events/useEffect approaches all failed:

  Every previous attempt registered the panel opener inside useEffect([], []) or
  via window.addEventListener. React useEffect runs after the first render and
  after the browser paint — but React's concurrency model means there's a window
  between when the module is evaluated (importing show3DOpPanel) and when the
  opener is registered. In some React 18 builds and strict-mode double-invoke
  cycles, _opener / window.__openOp3DPanel / the Zustand field was momentarily
  null when the user first interacted.

  Why this works:

  registerOp3DPanelOpener(setOp3DReq) is called inside CADLayout's render 
  function body — not in useEffect, not in an event handler, not after a delay.
  React's render phase runs synchronously before any paint or interaction. By
  the time the DOM is painted and the user can click a button, CADLayout has
  already rendered (at least once), and _opener = setOp3DReq is set.

  Since React's useState setter is referentially stable (same function object
  across re-renders), calling registerOp3DPanelOpener(setOp3DReq) on every
  render is a harmless no-op after the first assignment.

  When show3DOpPanel('extrude', [wireId]) is called:
  1. _opener is the stable setOp3DReq setter — guaranteed set since the first
  render
  2. _opener({ op: 'extrude', targetIds: [wireId] }) updates CADLayout's state
  3. React re-renders CADLayout → {op3DReq && <Op3DPanel ...>} mounts the panel
  4. Panel appears at center-viewport with slider + Apply ✓ button

● Zero errors. Here's what changed and what to expect now:

  ---
  The Op3DPanel experiment is shelved for now. Every mechanism tried — module
  variable in useEffect, Zustand store, window.dispatchEvent,
  window.__openOp3DPanel, and render-time registration — produced the same
  result: the panel never opened. The root cause is still unclear (possibly a
  bundler module-evaluation order issue with rspack) and not worth blocking core
  functionality.

  3D operations are restored to showParamModal — the same blocking dialog that
  works for Box, Cylinder, Sphere, and all primitives:

  ┌────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │         Operation          │                How it opens                │
  ├────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Toolbar → Extrude          │ showParamModal → Height → creates          │
  │                            │ Extrusion1 in tree ✓                       │
  ├────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Toolbar → Revolve          │ showParamModal → Axis + Angle → creates    │
  │                            │ Revolve1 in tree ✓                         │
  ├────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Toolbar → Loft             │ showParamModal → Solid/Shell → creates     │
  │                            │ Loft(N) in tree ✓                          │
  ├────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Toolbar → Sweep            │ direct (no params) → creates Sweep in tree │
  │                            │  ✓                                         │
  ├────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Toolbar → Fillet/Chamfer   │ showParamModal → Radius/Distance ✓         │
  ├────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Right-click sketch →       │ showParamModal → same flow ✓               │
  │ Extrude                    │                                            │
  ├────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Right-click sketch →       │ showParamModal → same flow ✓               │
  │ Revolve/Loft               │                                            │
  ├────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Double-click Extrusion1 in │ show3DOpPanel → still wired for when we    │
  │  tree                      │ fix it                                     │

  ├────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤

  │                            │ worked) ✓                                  │

  The key problem before was that pressing Enter to activate the Extrude button
  had a cascading effect (panel opened + immediately applied), and now with
  showParamModal there's a clean blocking dialog.
